Critical

Malicious Polymarket Bot Hides in Hijacked dev-protocol GitHub Org and Steals Wallet Keys

StepSecurity
2026-03-26 8 use cases 4 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

The StepSecurity threat intelligence team discovered that dev-protocol — a verified GitHub organization with 568 followers belonging to a legitimate Japanese DeFi project — has been hijacked and is now being used to distribute malicious Polymarket trading bots.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Malicious typosquat npm packages installed on disk (ts-bign / big-nunber / levex-refa / lint-builder)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Malicious Polymarket Bot Hides in Hijacked dev-protocol GitHub Org and Steals Wa
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] npm postinstall SSH-backdoor chain: node spawning sudo ufw allow 22/tcp + chown ~/.ssh
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] C2 beaconing to Vercel-hosted Cloudflare-impersonating domains (cloudflareguard / cloudflareinsights) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] C2 beaconing to Vercel-hosted Cloudflare-impersonating domains (cloudflareguard / cloudflareinsights) Command & Control High
Detects outbound HTTP(S) traffic or DNS resolution to the two Vercel-hosted C2 domains used by the malicious dev-protocol Polymarket bot supply chain — cloudflareguard.vercel.app (wallet/file exfil) and cloudflareinsights.vercel.app (C2 config + secondary exfil). These hostnames are campaign-exclusive and have no legitimate Cloudflare provenance despite their name. Rationale: Uses the two article-specific C2 hostnames cloudflareguard.vercel.app and cloudflareinsights.vercel.app verbatim — both are campaign-exclusive impersonations of Cloudflare naming. Three external sources (StepSecurity, GetSafety, Medium ADHD/Cyb3rhawk) corroborate the C2 infrastructure. Hostnames alone are sufficient because no legitimate Cloudflare service is hosted under those Vercel subdomains. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/malicious-polymarket-bot-hides-in-hijacked-dev-protocol-github-org-and-steals-wallet-keys • https://www.getsafety.com/blog-posts/polymarket-targeted-by-malicious-packages • https://medium.com/adhd-attack-detect-hunt-defend/infrastructure-pivoting-malicious-polymarket-npm-wallet-drainer-and-vidar-stealer-0b222f0e1a53
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1568 T1567 T1195.002
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Network_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web
let C2Domains = dynamic(["cloudflareguard.vercel.app","cloudflareinsights.vercel.app"]);
let C2UriPaths = dynamic(["/api/scan-patterns","/api/block-patterns","/api/v1"]);
union isfuzzy=true
(
  DeviceNetworkEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
  | where RemoteUrl has_any (C2Domains)
     or (RemoteUrl has_any (C2UriPaths) and RemoteUrl has ".vercel.app")
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, ActionType
),
(
  DeviceEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
  | where ActionType == "DnsQueryResponse"
  | extend Q = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).QueryName)
  | where Q has_any (C2Domains)
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Q
)
| order by Timestamp desc
let C2Domains = dynamic(["cloudflareguard.vercel.app","cloudflareinsights.vercel.app"]);
union isfuzzy=true
(
  DnsEvents
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where Name has_any (C2Domains)
  | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType, ResultCodeName
),
(
  ImDnsActivity
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where DnsQuery has_any (C2Domains)
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, DnsQuery, EventResult
),
(
  ImNetworkSession
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where DstHostname has_any (C2Domains) or Url has_any (C2Domains)
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, Url, DstPortNumber
),
(
  CommonSecurityLog
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where DestinationHostName has_any (C2Domains) or RequestURL has_any (C2Domains)
  | project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationHostName, RequestURL, RequestMethod
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 8c5d9a3b-2e7f-4f1a-9b8c-1d4e7a2c6f80
description: Detects DNS resolution or network connection to cloudflareguard.vercel.app or cloudflareinsights.vercel.app, the two C2 endpoints used by the malicious dev-protocol Polymarket trading bot supply chain attack (March 2026). Both domains are campaign-exclusive and impersonate Cloudflare service names.

[LLM] npm postinstall SSH-backdoor chain: node spawning sudo ufw allow 22/tcp + chown ~/.ssh Installation High
Detects the Linux/macOS post-install backdoor sequence emitted by lint-builder's test.js — a node process (typically spawned by npm/sh in a node_modules subdirectory) invoking sudo to enable ufw, open inbound port 22/tcp, and take ownership of the SSH key directory. This sequence is the lint-builder@1.0.1 fingerprint, not generic LOLBin/SSH hardening. Rationale: References the article's exact post-install chain: 'sudo ufw allow 22/tcp', 'sudo ufw enable', 'sudo chown -R runner:runner /home/runner/.ssh', all spawned from node test.js with working directory node_modules/lint-builder. Couples the destructive command with the npm-ancestor pivot so legitimate SSH hardening (rare) doesn't fire. Corroborated by StepSecurity process tree (PIDs 2407→2422→2428→2446→2447→2449→2450) and GetSafety write-up. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/malicious-polymarket-bot-hides-in-hijacked-dev-protocol-github-org-and-steals-wallet-keys • https://www.getsafety.com/blog-posts/polymarket-targeted-by-malicious-packages
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1546.016 T1098.004 T1562.004 T1059.004
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
let SuspectChildCmd = dynamic(["ufw allow 22","ufw allow 22/tcp","ufw enable"]);
let SshChownTokens = dynamic(["/.ssh","/home/runner/.ssh","authorized_keys"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where (ProcessCommandLine has_any (SuspectChildCmd))
    or (ProcessCommandLine has "chown" and ProcessCommandLine has_any (SshChownTokens) and ProcessCommandLine has "-R")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node","sh","bash","sudo","npm","dash")
    or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("node test.js","lint-builder","node_modules")
    or InitiatingProcessParentFileName =~ "node"
    or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has "node_modules"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ChildFile = FileName, ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          Parent = InitiatingProcessFileName, ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, ParentPath = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          GrandparentFile = InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc
let SuspectChildCmd = dynamic(["ufw allow 22","ufw allow 22/tcp","ufw enable"]);
let SshChownTokens = dynamic(["/.ssh","/home/runner/.ssh","authorized_keys"]);
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DvcOs has "Linux" or DvcOs has "Mac" or isempty(DvcOs)
| where (ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (SuspectChildCmd) or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (SuspectChildCmd))
    or ((ActingProcessCommandLine has "chown" or TargetProcessCommandLine has "chown")
        and (ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (SshChownTokens) or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (SshChownTokens)))
| where ParentProcessName in~ ("node","sh","bash","sudo","npm","dash")
    or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any ("node test.js","lint-builder","node_modules")
    or ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("node test.js","lint-builder","node_modules")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine,
          ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine,
          ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Polymarket lint-builder Postinstall SSH Backdoor Setup (ufw allow 22 + chown ~/.ssh from node)
id: 4f1e2b6c-9a8d-4e3f-b2c1-7e5a9d8b3c14
description: Detects the lint-builder@1.0.1 npm postinstall payload that opens an SSH backdoor on Linux/macOS via 'sudo ufw enable', 'sudo ufw allow 22/tcp', and 'sudo chown -R runner:runner /home/runner/.ssh', spawned from a node process executing test.js inside node_modules/lint-builder/. Part of the hijacked dev-protocol Polymarket trading bot supply chain attack (March 2026).
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/malicious-polymarket-bot-hides-in-hijacked-dev-protocol-github-org-and-steals-wallet-keys
  - https://www.getsafety.com/blog-posts/polymarket-targeted-by-malicious-packages
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/03/15
tags:
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.t1546.016
  - attack.t1098.004
  - attack.t1562.004
  - attack.t1059.004
  - attack.persistence
  - attack.defense_evasion
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: linux
detection:
  selection_ufw_open:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'ufw allow 22'
      - 'ufw allow 22/tcp'
  selection_chown_ssh:
    CommandLine|contains|all:
      - 'chown'
      - '-R'
    CommandLine|contains:
      - '/.ssh'
      - '/home/runner/.ssh'
      - 'authorized_keys'
  selection_ufw_enable:
    CommandLine|contains: 'ufw enable'
  ancestry_node_npm:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '/node'
      - '/sh'
      - '/bash'
      - '/sudo'
      - '/npm'
    ParentCommandLine|contains:
      - 'node test.js'
      - 'lint-builder'
      - 'node_modules'
  condition: (selection_ufw_open or selection_chown_ssh or selection_ufw_enable) and ancestry_node_npm
falsepositives:
  - Legitimate system administrators running SSH hardening playbooks (extremely rare from a node parent)
  - Containerised CI runners installing legitimate packages that bind port 22 (audit if observed)
level: high
source:linux.auditd @auditd.type:EXECVE (@process.command_line:(*"ufw allow 22"* OR *"ufw allow 22/tcp"* OR *"ufw enable"* OR (*chown* AND *-R* AND (*/.ssh* OR */home/runner/.ssh* OR *authorized_keys*)))) AND (@process.parent.name:(node OR sh OR bash OR sudo OR npm OR dash) OR @process.parent.command_line:(*"node test.js"* OR *lint-builder* OR *node_modules*))
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

  (Processes.process_name IN ("ufw","sudo") AND Processes.process="*ufw*allow*22*")
  OR (Processes.process_name IN ("chown","sudo") AND Processes.process="*chown*" AND Processes.process IN ("*/.ssh*","*/home/*/.ssh*"))
  OR (Processes.process_name IN ("ufw","sudo") AND Processes.process="*ufw*enable*")
) AND (Processes.parent_process_name IN ("node","sh","bash","npm","sudo") OR Processes.parent_process IN ("*node*test.js*","*lint-builder*","*node_modules*")) by host Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name Processes.parent_process | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | where match(parent_cmd,"(?i)(node|lint-builder|node_modules|test\.js)") OR match(parent_name,"(?i)^(node|sh|bash|sudo|npm)$")
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as cmd values(Processes.process_path) as path values(Processes.parent_process) as parent_cmd values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent_name from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.os IN ("Linux","macOS") AND (
  (Processes.process_name IN ("ufw","sudo") AND Processes.process="*ufw*allow*22*")
  OR (Processes.process_name IN ("chown","sudo") AND Processes.process="*chown*" AND Processes.process IN ("*/.ssh*","*/home/*/.ssh*"))
  OR (Processes.process_name IN ("ufw","sudo") AND Processes.process="*ufw*enable*")
) AND (Processes.parent_process_name IN ("node","sh","bash","npm","sudo") OR Processes.parent_process IN ("*node*test.js*","*lint-builder*","*node_modules*")) by host Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name Processes.parent_process | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | where match(parent_cmd,"(?i)(node|lint-builder|node_modules|test\.js)") OR match(parent_name,"(?i)^(node|sh|bash|sudo|npm)$")
[LLM] Malicious typosquat npm packages installed on disk (ts-bign / big-nunber / levex-refa / lint-builder) Delivery High
Hunts for filesystem evidence that any of the four named malicious npm packages from the dev-protocol Polymarket campaign were extracted under a node_modules directory. The four package names are typosquats of big.js and bignumber.js with no legitimate use — any presence in node_modules indicates a victim host that ran npm install against the hijacked repo. Rationale: All four package names (ts-bign, big-nunber, levex-refa, lint-builder) are typosquats explicitly named in the article and corroborated by GetSafety. They have no legitimate publisher on npm, so any node_modules path containing them is high-confidence evidence the host installed the malicious dev-protocol Polymarket repo. Used as a hunt-tier sweep to identify affected developer/CI hosts retroactively. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/malicious-polymarket-bot-hides-in-hijacked-dev-protocol-github-org-and-steals-wallet-keys • https://www.getsafety.com/blog-posts/polymarket-targeted-by-malicious-packages
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1608.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let MaliciousPkgs = dynamic(["ts-bign","big-nunber","levex-refa","lint-builder"]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has "node_modules"
| where FolderPath has_any (MaliciousPkgs)
| extend PkgName = case(
    FolderPath has "node_modules/ts-bign" or FolderPath has @"node_modules\ts-bign", "ts-bign",
    FolderPath has "node_modules/big-nunber" or FolderPath has @"node_modules\big-nunber", "big-nunber",
    FolderPath has "node_modules/levex-refa" or FolderPath has @"node_modules\levex-refa", "levex-refa",
    FolderPath has "node_modules/lint-builder" or FolderPath has @"node_modules\lint-builder", "lint-builder",
    "other")
| where PkgName != "other"
| summarize FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp),
            FileCount = count(), Samples = make_set(FileName, 10),
            CreatingProcess = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName, 5),
            CreatingCmd = make_set(InitiatingProcessCommandLine, 5)
            by DeviceName, PkgName
| order by FirstSeen desc
let MaliciousPkgs = dynamic(["ts-bign","big-nunber","levex-refa","lint-builder"]);
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where TargetFilePath has "node_modules"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (MaliciousPkgs)
| extend PkgName = case(
    TargetFilePath has "node_modules/ts-bign" or TargetFilePath has @"node_modules\ts-bign", "ts-bign",
    TargetFilePath has "node_modules/big-nunber" or TargetFilePath has @"node_modules\big-nunber", "big-nunber",
    TargetFilePath has "node_modules/levex-refa" or TargetFilePath has @"node_modules\levex-refa", "levex-refa",
    TargetFilePath has "node_modules/lint-builder" or TargetFilePath has @"node_modules\lint-builder", "lint-builder",
    "other")
| where PkgName != "other"
| summarize FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated),
            FileCount = count(),
            Samples = make_set(TargetFileName, 10),
            CreatingProcess = make_set(ActingProcessName, 5)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, PkgName
| order by FirstSeen desc

Any presence on a development or CI host indicates that npm install was run against the malicious repo.

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Critical

ForceMemo: Hundreds of GitHub Python Repos Compromised via Account Takeover and Force-Push

StepSecurity
2026-03-26 11 use cases 7 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

The StepSecurity threat intelligence team was the first to discover and report on an ongoing campaign — which we are tracking as ForceMemo — in which an attacker is compromising hundreds of GitHub accounts and injecting identical malware into hundreds of Python repositories. The earliest injections date to March 8, 2026, and the campaign is still active with new repos continuing to be compromised.

ATT&CKT1027 T1176 T1190 T1195.002 T1204.002 T1539 T1555.003

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — ForceMemo: Hundreds of GitHub Python Repos Compromised via Account Takeover and
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] ForceMemo: Node.js v22.9.0 spawned by Python from user home directory · [LLM] ForceMemo: init.json persistence file or i.js loader dropped by Python in user home root · Suspicious browser extension installation
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] ForceMemo: Python process queries Solana mainnet RPC endpoint (blockchain dead-drop C2) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
[LLM] ForceMemo: Node.js v22.9.0 spawned by Python from user home directory Installation High
Hunts the ForceMemo payload-execution stage where the obfuscated Python stub downloads Node.js v22.9.0 from nodejs.org, extracts it under the user's home directory, and then invokes the bundled node binary to run the AES-encrypted JS stage. A node binary inside a 'node-v22.9.0-*' folder under a user home, spawned by a python/pip parent, is highly specific to this campaign. Rationale: The 'node-v22.9.0' string is verbatim from StepSecurity's Harden-Runner capture (`/home/runner/node-v22.9.0-linux-x64/bin/node`) and is hard-coded into the malware. Pairing that path with a python/pip parent eliminates any benign Node.js installation. Cross-checked against The Hacker News, SecurityWeek and SCWorld write-ups which all reproduce the same Node.js v22.9.0 download + home-directory extraction behaviour. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/03/glassworm-attack-uses-stolen-github.html • https://www.securityweek.com/forcememo-python-repositories-compromised-in-glassworm-aftermath/ • https://www.scworld.com/brief/glassworm-campaign-evolves-forcememo-attack-targets-python-repositories-via-stolen-github-tokens • https://www.rescana.com/post/glassworm-forcememo-campaign-supply-chain-attack-targets-github-python-repositories-with-stolen-tok/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.006 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName has_any ("python","python.exe","python3","python3.10","python3.11","python3.12","python3.13","pip","pip.exe","pip3")
| where FileName in~ ("node","node.exe")
| where FolderPath has "node-v22.9.0"
| where FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)(\\Users\\[^\\]+\\node-v22\.9\.0-|/home/[^/]+/node-v22\.9\.0-|/Users/[^/]+/node-v22\.9\.0-|/root/node-v22\.9\.0-)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("python","python3","python.exe","pip","pip.exe","pip3")
| where TargetProcessName has "node-v22.9.0"
| where TargetProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\Users\\[^\\]+\\node-v22\.9\.0-|/home/[^/]+/node-v22\.9\.0-|/Users/[^/]+/node-v22\.9\.0-|/root/node-v22\.9\.0-)"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: ForceMemo Node.js v22.9.0 Spawned by Python from User Home Directory
id: 8f3a2b4d-1e5c-4a7b-9d6f-2c8e1a4b5c7d
status: experimental
description: Detects ForceMemo malware's payload execution stage, where a compromised setup.py / main.py / app.py downloads Node.js v22.9.0 from nodejs.org into the user home directory and spawns the bundled node binary to run an AES-encrypted JavaScript stage.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/forcememo-hundreds-of-github-python-repos-compromised-via-account-takeover-and-force-push
  - https://thehackernews.com/2026/03/glassworm-attack-uses-stolen-github.html
  - https://www.securityweek.com/forcememo-python-repositories-compromised-in-glassworm-aftermath/
author: thn-usecases
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.t1059.006
  - attack.t1105
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '\\python.exe'
      - '/python'
      - '/python3'
      - '/python3.10'
      - '/python3.11'
      - '/python3.12'
      - '/python3.13'
      - '\\pip.exe'
      - '/pip'
      - '/pip3'
  selection_child:
    Image|contains:
      - '\\node-v22.9.0-'
      - '/node-v22.9.0-'
    Image|endswith:
      - '\\node.exe'
      - '/node'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child
falsepositives:
  - Very unlikely; Python projects do not normally bundle and execute Node.js v22.9.0 from inside the user home directory.
level: high

(source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\\python.exe OR *\\Python.exe OR *\\python3*.exe OR *\\pip.exe OR *\\Pip.exe OR *\\pip3.exe) @Image:(*\\node-v22.9.0-*\\node.exe OR *\\Node-v22.9.0-*\\node.exe OR *\\NODE-V22.9.0-*\\node.exe)) OR (source:linux.auditd @auditd.type:EXECVE @process.executable.path:*node-v22.9.0-*/bin/node)

[LLM] ForceMemo: Python process queries Solana mainnet RPC endpoint (blockchain dead-drop C2) Command & Control High
Hunts the ForceMemo blockchain C2 lookup. After execution, the malware queries the on-chain memo at Solana wallet BjVeAjPrSKFiingBn4vZvghsGj9KCE8AJVtbc9S8o8SC via one of 9 named Solana RPC providers, fetching its current payload URL. A Python (or pip) process contacting any of these RPC endpoints is high-fidelity for non-crypto orgs. Rationale: All 9 RPC FQDNs are verbatim from the StepSecurity write-up and are hard-coded in the malware as a resilient fallback chain. The Solana C2 wallet (BjVeAjPrSKF...8o8SC) is corroborated on-chain and across The Hacker News / SecurityWeek / SCWorld / Rescana. Pairing the destination with a python/pip parent removes the only realistic FP class (legit Web3 devs). Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/03/glassworm-attack-uses-stolen-github.html • https://www.securityweek.com/forcememo-python-repositories-compromised-in-glassworm-aftermath/ • https://www.rescana.com/post/glassworm-forcememo-campaign-supply-chain-attack-targets-github-python-repositories-with-stolen-tok/ • https://cybersecuritynews.com/forcememo-hijacks-github-accounts/
ATT&CKT1102.001 T1071.001 T1195.002
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution Network_Traffic
let SolanaRpc = dynamic(["api.mainnet-beta.solana.com","solana-mainnet.gateway.tatum.io","go.getblock.us","solana-rpc.publicnode.com","api.blockeden.xyz","solana.drpc.org","solana.leorpc.com","solana.api.onfinality.io","solana.api.pocket.network"]);
union
  ( DeviceNetworkEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName has_any ("python","python.exe","python3","python3.10","python3.11","python3.12","python3.13","pip","pip.exe","pip3")
    | where RemoteUrl has_any (SolanaRpc)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="Network", InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName ),
  ( DeviceEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where ActionType == "DnsQueryResponse"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName has_any ("python","python.exe","python3","python3.10","python3.11","python3.12","python3.13","pip","pip.exe","pip3")
    | extend Query = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).QueryName)
    | where Query in~ (SolanaRpc)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, Source="DNS", InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl=Query, RemoteIP, RemotePort=int(null), AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName )
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] ForceMemo: init.json persistence file or i.js loader dropped by Python in user home root Installation Medium
Hunts the ForceMemo persistence and JS-loader artefacts. The Python stub writes a JavaScript decryption loader as 'i.js' and a 'init.json' state file with a 2-day recheck timer directly into the user's home directory root. Both filenames are short and generic, but the combination of (python/pip parent) + (filename) + (path = home-directory root, not nested) is highly specific. Rationale: Filenames 'i.js' and 'init.json' and their direct-in-home-root location are verbatim from the StepSecurity write-up ('Writes a JS file (i.js)... Creates a persistence file (~/init.json) with a 2-day recheck timer'). The (python parent) + (home-root, not nested) regex eliminates almost all benign 'init.json' creations, which typically live in app-config or package subdirs. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2026/03/glassworm-attack-uses-stolen-github.html • https://www.securityweek.com/forcememo-python-repositories-compromised-in-glassworm-aftermath/ • https://gbhackers.com/forcememo-hijacks-github/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1027 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where ActionType == "FileCreated"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName has_any ("python","python.exe","python3","python3.10","python3.11","python3.12","python3.13","pip","pip.exe","pip3")
| where FileName in~ ("init.json","i.js")
| where FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)^([A-Z]:\\Users\\[^\\]+\\?|/home/[^/]+/?|/Users/[^/]+/?|/root/?)$"
| where FolderPath !has "site-packages" and FolderPath !has "node_modules" and FolderPath !has ".venv" and FolderPath !has "venv"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where EventType == "FileCreated"
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("python","python3","python.exe","pip","pip.exe","pip3")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("init.json","i.js")
| where TargetFilePath matches regex @"(?i)^([A-Z]:\\Users\\[^\\]+\\(init\.json|i\.js)|/home/[^/]+/(init\.json|i\.js)|/Users/[^/]+/(init\.json|i\.js)|/root/(init\.json|i\.js))$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

High

xygeni-action Compromised: C2 Reverse Shell Backdoor Injected via Tag Poisoning

StepSecurity
2026-03-26 8 use cases 3 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

The official Xygeni GitHub Action (xygeni-action) was compromised on March 3, 2026, when an attacker using stolen maintainer credentials injected a full C2 reverse shell backdoor and silently moved the mutable v5 tag to the malicious commit - affecting all repositories referencing @v5 without any visible change to their workflow files. The v5 tag remains poisoned as of March 9; users should immediately pin to v6.4.0 or a specific commit SHA, and StepSecurity's Harden-Runner would have detected and blocked the C2 callback to 91.214.78.178.

ATT&CKT1027 T1190 T1195.002
Domainssecurity-verify.91.214.78.178.nip.io
IPs91.214.78.178

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] GitHub Actions workflow file referencing compromised xygeni/xygeni-action@v5 or backdoored commit 4bf1d4e
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → Article-specific behavioural hunt — xygeni-action Compromised: C2 Reverse Shell Backdoor Injected via Tag Poisoning
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Outbound C2 callback to xygeni-action backdoor IP 91.214.78.178 from CI runner · [LLM] Bash-spawned curl to xygeni-action C2 nip.io endpoint with /b/in /b/q /b/r path on CI runner · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Outbound C2 callback to xygeni-action backdoor IP 91.214.78.178 from CI runner Command & Control High
Detects any outbound connection from a host (typically a CI/CD runner) to the xygeni-action C2 infrastructure: the literal IP 91.214.78.178 or any nip.io wildcard subdomain wrapping that IP (e.g. security-verify.91.214.78.178.nip.io). The implant registers via POST /b/in then polls /b/q every 2-7s for 3 minutes. Rationale: Anchored on the verbatim C2 IP (91.214.78.178) and the nip.io wildcard FQDN (security-verify.91.214.78.178.nip.io) named in the article. The IP belongs to attacker-controlled infrastructure and nip.io subdomains embedding that IP only exist for the purpose of evading IP-block lists; no legitimate enterprise traffic should resolve them. Cross-checked with StepSecurity's incident write-up. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/xygeni-action-compromised-c2-reverse-shell-backdoor-injected-via-tag-poisoning
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1090 T1568.002
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteIP == "91.214.78.178"
   or RemoteUrl has "91.214.78.178.nip.io"
   or RemoteUrl has "security-verify.91.214.78.178"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
let MaliciousIP = "91.214.78.178";
let MaliciousFqdn = "91.214.78.178.nip.io";
union isfuzzy=true
(ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DstIpAddr == MaliciousIP or DstHostname has MaliciousFqdn or Url has MaliciousFqdn
    | extend Host = DvcHostname, Src = SrcHostname, DstIp = DstIpAddr, DstHost = DstHostname, DstPort = tostring(DstPortNumber), MatchUrl = Url
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceTable = "ImNetworkSession", Host, Src, DstIp, DstHost, DstPort, MatchUrl),
(CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DestinationIP == MaliciousIP or DestinationHostName has MaliciousFqdn or RequestURL has MaliciousFqdn
    | extend Host = SourceHostName, Src = SourceIP, DstIp = DestinationIP, DstHost = DestinationHostName, DstPort = tostring(DestinationPort), MatchUrl = RequestURL
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceTable = "CommonSecurityLog", Host, Src, DstIp, DstHost, DstPort, MatchUrl),
(Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SyslogMessage has MaliciousIP or SyslogMessage has "91.214.78.178.nip.io" or SyslogMessage has "security-verify.91.214.78.178"
    | extend Host = HostName, Src = HostName, DstIp = MaliciousIP, DstHost = "", DstPort = "", MatchUrl = SyslogMessage
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceTable = "Syslog", Host, Src, DstIp, DstHost, DstPort, MatchUrl)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`

title: Obfuscated / Encoded PowerShell Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.execution
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
    selection_args:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")

status: experimental

    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:

    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'

            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'

        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:

      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high

[LLM] Malicious litellm 1.82.7/1.82.8 wheel install drops litellm_init.pth in site-packages Delivery High
Detects installation of the trojanized litellm PyPI versions 1.82.7 or 1.82.8 by spotting the diagnostic file litellm_init.pth being written into a Python site-packages directory (an unusual artefact name for legitimate packages) and/or pip writing litellm-1.82.7/litellm-1.82.8 .dist-info metadata. Versions 1.82.6 and earlier are clean. Rationale: Anchored on the article's explicit artefact litellm_init.pth (34,628 bytes) dropped into site-packages and the two malicious wheel versions 1.82.7 / 1.82.8. The file name is novel to this campaign and the dist-info paths are version-pinned, giving high fidelity. Cross-checked: PyPI advisory SNYK-PYTHON-LITELLM-15762713 and the FutureSearch disclosure both confirm the file name and sizes. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-PYTHON-LITELLM-15762713 • https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/issues/24518
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1546.018
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName =~ "litellm_init.pth" and FolderPath has "site-packages")
   or (FolderPath has_any ("litellm-1.82.7.dist-info", "litellm-1.82.8.dist-info"))
   or (FileName =~ "litellm_init.pth" and FileSize between (30000 .. 40000))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, FileSize, SHA256,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Facility in ("auth", "daemon", "user") or isnotempty(SyslogMessage)
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("litellm_init.pth", "litellm-1.82.7.dist-info", "litellm-1.82.8.dist-info", "litellm-1.82.7-py3", "litellm-1.82.8-py3")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostName, ProcessName, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Python .pth startup hook executes subprocess to curl C2 (litellm fork-bomb pattern) Installation High
Detects the .pth-triggered execution chain unique to litellm 1.82.8: python(1) spawning python(2) (because the .pth re-fires on each interpreter), which in turn spawns curl or openssl to package and POST tpcp.tar.gz to models.litellm.cloud. The python→python→curl/openssl tree is what produced the RAM-exhaustion symptom McMahon reported. Rationale: Article-specific anchors: (a) python parent — because the .pth fires from the interpreter, (b) curl/openssl/tar child — the verbatim exfil stack named in the article, (c) the literal strings models.litellm.cloud, tpcp.tar.gz, session.key.enc, payload.enc. Generic python-spawns-curl is too noisy; the three IOC strings give the article-specific lift. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/poisoned-security-scanner-backdooring-litellm/ • https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/issues/24518
ATT&CKT1546.018 T1059.006
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("python","python3","python.exe","python3.exe")
| where (FileName in~ ("curl","curl.exe","openssl","openssl.exe","tar","tar.exe"))
   and (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","tpcp.tar.gz","session.key","payload.enc","session.key.enc")
        or ProcessCommandLine has "AES-256-CBC" and ProcessCommandLine has "-pbkdf2")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Facility in ("authpriv","auth","user","daemon")
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","tpcp.tar.gz","session.key.enc","payload.enc","litellm_init.pth")
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("curl","openssl","python")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostName, ProcessName, ProcessID, SyslogMessage
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Python Spawned curl/openssl Posting tpcp.tar.gz to litellm Exfil Domain
id: 7b9c4a13-1f8b-4d23-9c44-22c4a8e0fbd2
description: Python process spawning curl or openssl with command-line references to models.litellm.cloud or the TeamPCP exfil artefacts (tpcp.tar.gz, session.key, payload.enc).
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/poisoned-security-scanner-backdooring-litellm/
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/03/24
tags:
  - attack.t1546.018
  - attack.t1041
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '/python'
      - '/python3'
      - '\python.exe'
      - '\python3.exe'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/curl'
      - '\curl.exe'
      - '/openssl'
      - '\openssl.exe'
      - '/tar'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'models.litellm.cloud'
      - 'tpcp.tar.gz'
      - 'session.key.enc'
      - 'payload.enc'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
level: critical
source:(windows.sysmon OR linux.auditd) (@ParentImage:(*python* OR *PYTHON*) OR @process.parent.executable.path:*python*) (@Image:(*curl* OR *openssl* OR *tar*) OR @process.executable.path:(*curl* OR *openssl* OR *tar*)) (@CommandLine:(*models.litellm.cloud* OR *tpcp.tar.gz* OR *session.key.enc* OR *payload.enc*) OR @process.command_line:(*models.litellm.cloud* OR *tpcp.tar.gz* OR *session.key.enc* OR *payload.enc*))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| ParentBaseFileName=/^python(3)?(\.exe)?$/i
| ImageFileName=/(curl|openssl|tar)(\.exe)?$/i
| regex("(models\.litellm\.cloud|tpcp\.tar\.gz|session\.key\.enc|payload\.enc)", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

[LLM] DNS / HTTPS egress to TeamPCP exfil infra (models.litellm.cloud, checkmarx.zone) Command & Control High
Detects any host in the org resolving or connecting to the two TeamPCP-controlled exfil domains used across the litellm, Trivy, and Checkmarx KICS operations. Both domains were registered in March 2026 (models.litellm.cloud on Mar 23) — any resolution within or after that window is highly suspicious. Rationale: Direct IOC match on the exfil and C2 domains/IP that Wiz, Endor Labs, and Snyk all corroborate (same RSA key fingerprint across litellm/Trivy/KICS payloads). These domains have no benign legitimate use — registered Mar 23 2026 specifically for this campaign — so any hit is high-fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/poisoned-security-scanner-backdooring-litellm/ • https://www.endorlabs.com/learn/teampcp • https://www.wiz.io/blog/teampcp-supply-chain

( DeviceNetworkEvents

  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
),
( DeviceEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(60d)
  | where ActionType == "DnsConnectionInspected" or ActionType has "Dns"
  | where RemoteUrl has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","checkmarx.zone") or AdditionalFields has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","checkmarx.zone")
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName, RemoteUrl, AdditionalFields
)
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
( DnsEvents
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
  | where Name has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","checkmarx.zone")
  | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType, ResultCode
),
( CommonSecurityLog
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
  | where DestinationHostName has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","checkmarx.zone") or RequestURL has_any ("models.litellm.cloud","checkmarx.zone") or DestinationIP == "83.142.209.11"

| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Exfil staging artefacts: session.key, payload.enc, session.key.enc, tpcp.tar.gz in temp Actions on Objectives High
Detects the four staging files the TeamPCP payload creates in the system temp directory while building the encrypted exfil bundle (AES-256-CBC session key wrapped with their RSA-4096 OAEP public key, archived as tpcp.tar.gz). All four names are hardcoded in the malware and shared across litellm / Trivy / KICS operations. Rationale: All four filenames (tpcp.tar.gz, session.key, payload.enc, session.key.enc) are quoted verbatim from the article's Stage 2 description. Wiz confirmed identical RSA public key across litellm/Trivy/KICS, meaning these staging names are reused across all three campaigns — extremely low FP rate. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/poisoned-security-scanner-backdooring-litellm/ • https://www.wiz.io/blog/teampcp-supply-chain
ATT&CKT1560.001 T1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("tpcp.tar.gz","session.key","session.key.enc","payload.enc")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp),
            Files=make_set(FileName), DistinctFiles=dcount(FileName),
            Procs=make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName),
            CmdLines=make_set(InitiatingProcessCommandLine),
            Paths=make_set(FolderPath)
          by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| where DistinctFiles >= 2 or array_index_of(Files, "tpcp.tar.gz") >= 0
| order by FirstSeen desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("tpcp.tar.gz","session.key.enc","payload.enc")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostName, ProcessName, ProcessID, SyslogMessage
| summarize FirstSeen=min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen=max(TimeGenerated),
            Procs=make_set(ProcessName), MsgSample=any(SyslogMessage),
            Hits=count()
          by Computer, HostName
| order by FirstSeen desc

[LLM] TeamPCP Linux credential harvest: Python reading /etc/shadow + auth.log + cloud Actions on Objectives Medium
Detects the Stage 1 collection behaviour: a python process opening a high-fan-out set of sensitive Linux credential stores (/etc/shadow, /var/log/auth.log, ~/.ssh/, ~/.aws/, ~/.azure/, ~/.config/gcloud/, ~/.kube/, ~/.docker/config.json, .env, cryptocurrency wallets) within a short window — characteristic of the litellm 1.82.7/1.82.8 grabber, not a normal interpreter session. Rationale: The article lists the exact files/paths the Stage 1 grabber touches; we anchor on that high-fan-out set (≥3 distinct sensitive paths from a python interpreter within 7d) rather than any single path, which avoids generic /etc/shadow noise from sysadmins. The IMDS string 169.254.169.254 is included because the article explicitly calls out direct IMDS queries. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/poisoned-security-scanner-backdooring-litellm/
ATT&CKT1003.008 T1552.001 T1552.004 T1552.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
let SensitivePaths = dynamic([
    "/etc/shadow", "/var/log/auth.log",
    "/.ssh/id_", "/.ssh/authorized_keys",
    "/.aws/credentials", "/.aws/config",
    "/.azure/", "/.config/gcloud/",
    "/.kube/config", "/.docker/config.json",
    "/.env", "wallet.dat", "/.bitcoin/", "/.ethereum/keystore/", "/.solana/", "/.cardano/", "/Ledger/"
  ]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("python","python3")
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed","FileAccessed")
   or ActionType has "File"
| extend MatchedPath = tostring(SensitivePaths[0])
| extend Hits = array_length(set_intersect(SensitivePaths, pack_array(FolderPath)))
| where FolderPath has_any (SensitivePaths) or FileName has_any ("shadow","auth.log","credentials","kubeconfig","wallet.dat")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp),
            DistinctSensitivePaths=dcount(FolderPath),
            PathsSample=make_set(FolderPath, 25),
            Cmd=any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
          by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessId
| where DistinctSensitivePaths >= 3
| order by FirstSeen desc
Syslog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where Facility in ("auth","authpriv","daemon","user","kern")
| where ProcessName matches regex @"(?i)^python[0-9]?$"
   or SyslogMessage has_any ("python","python3")
| where SyslogMessage has_any ("/etc/shadow","/var/log/auth.log","/.ssh/id_","/.aws/credentials","/.aws/config","/.azure/","/.config/gcloud/","/.kube/config","/.docker/config.json","wallet.dat","/.bitcoin/","/.ethereum/keystore/","169.254.169.254")
| summarize FirstSeen=min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen=max(TimeGenerated),
            Hits=count(),
            DistinctIndicators=dcount(extract(@"(/etc/shadow|/var/log/auth\.log|/\.ssh/id_|/\.aws/credentials|/\.aws/config|/\.azure/|/\.config/gcloud/|/\.kube/config|/\.docker/config\.json|wallet\.dat|/\.bitcoin/|/\.ethereum/keystore/|169\.254\.169\.254)", 0, SyslogMessage)),
            Sample=any(SyslogMessage),
            Procs=make_set(ProcessName)
          by Computer, HostName
| where DistinctIndicators >= 3
| order by FirstSeen desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

          AccessMask, AccessReason

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url

| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")

     | rename dest AS src]

          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Cloudflare-tunnel curl-piped Python stager (kamikaze.sh / kube.py)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — CanisterWorm Gets Teeth: TeamPCP's Kubernetes Wiper Targets Iran
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Malicious privileged DaemonSet apply in kube-system (host-provisioner-iran / host-provisioner-std / kamikaze) · [LLM] CanisterWorm persistence: pglog/pg_state/internal-monitor systemd unit and /tmp/pglog drop · RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] DNS/HTTP egress to CanisterWorm ICP canister C2 (tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Host-root mount wiper: chroot /mnt/host reboot -f or rm -rf / --no-preserve-root · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] DNS/HTTP egress to CanisterWorm ICP canister C2 (tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai) Command & Control High
Hunts for lookups or connections to the TeamPCP CanisterWorm Internet Computer Protocol canister at tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai.raw.icp0.io. The reused canister is the C2 endpoint that the Kubernetes wiper variant's installed backdoor polls every 50 minutes, so any DNS or HTTP touch is a high-confidence indicator of TeamPCP presence. Rationale: The canister ID tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai is unique to TeamPCP's CanisterWorm family (Aikido + earlier CanisterWorm writeups), so any traffic to that subdomain is essentially zero-FP. The ICP raw subdomain is the documented poll path; canister reuse across the Trivy compromise and Kubernetes variants is corroborated in the Aikido CanisterWorm post. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/teampcp-stage-payload-canisterworm-iran • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1102/002/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004 T1102.002
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Web.Web
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has_any ("tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai", ".raw.icp0.io")
   or AdditionalFields has "tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl, LocalIP
| order by Timestamp desc
let canister = "tdtqy-oyaaa-aaaae-af2dq-cai";
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImDnsActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DnsQuery has canister or DnsQuery endswith ".raw.icp0.io"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = DvcHostname, SrcHost = SrcHostname, SrcIp = SrcIpAddr, Query = DnsQuery, Vendor = EventVendor),
  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Name has canister or Name endswith ".raw.icp0.io"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = Computer, SrcHost = "", SrcIp = ClientIP, Query = Name, Vendor = "MicrosoftDNS"),
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where RequestURL has canister or DestinationHostName has canister or DestinationDnsDomain has canister
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = SourceHostName, SrcHost = SourceHostName, SrcIp = SourceIP, Query = coalesce(RequestURL, DestinationHostName, DestinationDnsDomain), Vendor = DeviceVendor),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SyslogMessage has canister
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = Computer, SrcHost = HostName, SrcIp = HostIP, Query = SyslogMessage, Vendor = "Syslog")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Cloudflare-tunnel curl-piped Python stager (kamikaze.sh / kube.py) Delivery High
Hunts for the TeamPCP wiper stager that downloads kamikaze.sh / kube.py from a trycloudflare.com tunnel and pipes the output into python3 (`curl -L -s <url> | python3 -`). Anchored on the verbatim filenames and tunnel hostnames Aikido observed. Rationale: Anchored on the verbatim stager URLs and filenames (kamikaze.sh, kube.py, both Cloudflare tunnel hostnames) Aikido captured, combined with the curl/wget|python3 binary set. Legitimate use of these exact filenames from these exact tunnels is implausible. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/teampcp-stage-payload-canisterworm-iran • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1105/
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1105 T1102
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("kamikaze.sh","kube.py","souls-entire-defined-routes.trycloudflare.com","championships-peoples-point-cassette.trycloudflare.com")
   or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("kamikaze.sh","kube.py","souls-entire-defined-routes.trycloudflare.com","championships-peoples-point-cassette.trycloudflare.com")
| where FileName in~ ("curl","wget","python","python3","bash","sh","dash")
     or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("curl","wget","python","python3","bash","sh","dash")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
let iocs = dynamic(["kamikaze.sh","kube.py","souls-entire-defined-routes.trycloudflare.com","championships-peoples-point-cassette.trycloudflare.com"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (iocs) or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (iocs) or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any (iocs)
    | where ActingProcessName in~ ("curl","wget","python","python3","bash","sh","dash")
         or TargetProcessName in~ ("curl","wget","python","python3","bash","sh","dash")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = DvcHostname, User = ActorUsername, Proc = coalesce(TargetProcessName, ActingProcessName), Cmd = coalesce(TargetProcessCommandLine, ActingProcessCommandLine), Parent = ParentProcessName),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SyslogMessage has_any (iocs)
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = Computer, User = "", Proc = ProcessName, Cmd = SyslogMessage, Parent = "")
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: TeamPCP Cloudflare Tunnel kamikaze/kube Stager Download

[LLM] Malicious privileged DaemonSet apply in kube-system (host-provisioner-iran / host-provisioner-std / kamikaze) Installation High
Hunts for creation of the TeamPCP DaemonSets named host-provisioner-iran (wiper) or host-provisioner-std (backdoor installer) in kube-system, including the privileged hostPath `/` mount and kamikaze container name. Detects at apply time so the iran variant can be stopped before chroot reboot fires across nodes. Rationale: Anchored on three article-verbatim DaemonSet/container names (host-provisioner-iran, host-provisioner-std, kamikaze) with no legitimate counterpart, plus the privileged + hostPath + /mnt/host pattern unique to host-escape containers. Detects at apply time — before the wiper container schedules and chroot reboot fires. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/teampcp-stage-payload-canisterworm-iran • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1610/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1611/
ATT&CKT1610 T1611 T1485
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Change.All_Changes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName =~ "kubectl" or InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "kubectl")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("host-provisioner-iran","host-provisioner-std","kamikaze")
   or (ProcessCommandLine has "apply" and ProcessCommandLine has "kube-system" and ProcessCommandLine has "DaemonSet")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
AKSAudit
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Verb in ("create","apply","patch","update")
| where RequestUri has "daemonsets" and RequestUri has "kube-system"
| where tostring(RequestObject) has_any ("host-provisioner-iran","host-provisioner-std","kamikaze")
   or (tostring(RequestObject) has "privileged" and tostring(RequestObject) has "hostPath" and tostring(RequestObject) has "/mnt/host")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Username, User, Verb, Method, RequestUri, ResponseStatus, ObjectRef, RequestObject, _ResourceId
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Host-root mount wiper: chroot /mnt/host reboot -f or rm -rf / --no-preserve-root Actions on Objectives High
Hunts for execution of the TeamPCP wiper commands inside a privileged container or directly on the host: `find /mnt/host -maxdepth 1 -not -name 'mnt' -exec rm -rf` followed by `chroot /mnt/host reboot -f`, or the non-K8s poison-pill `rm -rf / --no-preserve-root`. Rationale: Three command-line signatures pulled verbatim from the kamikaze container args and poison_pill() function in kube.py. None of these strings have a legitimate operational counterpart — `--no-preserve-root` exists specifically to override the safety check, and `chroot /mnt/host reboot -f` is only seen when a privileged container is forcibly rebooting its host. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/teampcp-stage-payload-canisterworm-iran • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1485/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1611/
ATT&CKT1485 T1561.002 T1611 T1529
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has "rm -rf / --no-preserve-root"
   or (ProcessCommandLine has "chroot" and ProcessCommandLine has "/mnt/host" and ProcessCommandLine has "reboot")
   or (ProcessCommandLine has "find /mnt/host" and ProcessCommandLine has "-exec rm -rf")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine has "rm -rf / --no-preserve-root"
       or (ActingProcessCommandLine has "chroot" and ActingProcessCommandLine has "/mnt/host" and ActingProcessCommandLine has "reboot")
       or (ActingProcessCommandLine has "find /mnt/host" and ActingProcessCommandLine has "-exec rm -rf")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = DvcHostname, User = ActorUsername, Proc = ActingProcessName, Cmd = ActingProcessCommandLine, Parent = ParentProcessName),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SyslogMessage has "rm -rf / --no-preserve-root"
       or (SyslogMessage has "chroot" and SyslogMessage has "/mnt/host" and SyslogMessage has "reboot")
       or (SyslogMessage has "find /mnt/host" and SyslogMessage has "-exec rm -rf")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = Computer, User = "", Proc = ProcessName, Cmd = SyslogMessage, Parent = "")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

description: Detects the destructive payload from TeamPCP's CanisterWorm Kubernetes wiper variant — either the in-container chroot+reboot pattern or the bare-metal poison-pill rm -rf with --no-preserve-root

  category: process_creation

      - 'reboot'

| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentBaseFileName], function=count())
