[LLM] CanisterWorm persistence: pglog/pg_state/internal-monitor systemd unit and /tmp/pglog drop Installation High
Hunts for the CanisterWorm backdoor's host-side persistence — the runner.py drop into /tmp/pglog (CONFIG['TARGET_DIR']) and the systemd unit named pglog / pg_state / internal-monitor (PostgreSQL-themed decoy names) wired with `ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3 .../runner.py` and `Description=System Monitor`. Rationale: Article-verbatim service names (pglog, pg_state, internal-monitor — PostgreSQL-themed masquerade per Aikido + earlier CanisterWorm post), drop path (/tmp/pglog), and the unique unit fields `Description=System Monitor` + `ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3 .../runner.py`. No legitimate PostgreSQL tooling exists at those names with that ExecStart binary. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/teampcp-stage-payload-canisterworm-iran • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1543/002/
ATT&CKT1543.002 T1036.005 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
union isfuzzy=true
  (DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where (FolderPath has "/etc/systemd/system/" and FileName in~ ("pglog.service","pg_state.service","internal-monitor.service"))
        or FolderPath startswith "/tmp/pglog"
        or (FolderPath has "/tmp/" and FileName =~ "runner.py")
    | extend Evt = "FileWrite", Detail = strcat(FolderPath, FileName), Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Bin = InitiatingProcessFileName),
  (DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("systemctl enable --now pglog","systemctl enable --now pg_state","systemctl enable --now internal-monitor")
         or (ProcessCommandLine has "Description=System Monitor" and ProcessCommandLine has "ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3")
    | extend Evt = "ProcessExec", Detail = ProcessCommandLine, Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Bin = FileName)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, Evt, Detail, Bin, Cmd
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where (TargetFilePath has "/etc/systemd/system/" and TargetFileName in~ ("pglog.service","pg_state.service","internal-monitor.service"))
        or TargetFilePath startswith "/tmp/pglog"
        or (TargetFilePath has "/tmp/" and TargetFileName =~ "runner.py")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = DvcHostname, User = ActorUsername, Evt = "FileWrite", Detail = TargetFilePath, Proc = ActingProcessName, Cmd = ActingProcessCommandLine),
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("systemctl enable --now pglog","systemctl enable --now pg_state","systemctl enable --now internal-monitor")
         or (ActingProcessCommandLine has "Description=System Monitor" and ActingProcessCommandLine has "ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = DvcHostname, User = ActorUsername, Evt = "ProcessExec", Detail = ActingProcessCommandLine, Proc = ActingProcessName, Cmd = ActingProcessCommandLine),
  (Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where SyslogMessage has_any ("pglog.service","pg_state.service","internal-monitor.service")
        and SyslogMessage has_any ("systemd","enabled","started")
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = Computer, User = "", Evt = "SyslogUnit", Detail = SyslogMessage, Proc = ProcessName, Cmd = SyslogMessage)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore files from MetaMask,
    Exodus, Atomic, Phantom, Bitcoin, Electrum, Ethereum-cli wallets.
    Filtering out the legitimate wallet binaries leaves only attacker
    activity.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1005/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1005
    - attack.collection
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Ethereum\keystore\'
            - '\Bitcoin\'
            - '\Exodus\'
            - '\Electrum\wallets\'
            - '\MetaMask\'
            - '\Phantom\'
            - '\Atomic\Local Storage\'
    filter_legit:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\MetaMask.exe'
            - '\Exodus.exe'
            - '\Atomic.exe'
            - '\electrum.exe'
            - '\Bitcoin.exe'
            - '\Phantom.exe'
    condition: selection_path and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
    - Backup software
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")

// article (matching shape with the Defender query).

   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"

id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)

    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'

        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor

      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.

ATT&CKT1204.002

// Article-specific bespoke detection — CanisterWorm Gets Teeth: TeamPCP's Kubernetes Wiper Targets Iran
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("kube.py", "pgmon.py", "proxy.sh", "pcpcat.py"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FolderPath has_any ("/tmp/pglog", "/usr/bin/env", "/dev/null", "/tmp/kubectl", "/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount", "/etc/timezone", "/usr/bin/python3", "/var/lib/svc_internal/runner.py") or FileName in~ ("kube.py", "pgmon.py", "proxy.sh", "pcpcat.py"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

      AND (Filesystem.file_path="*/tmp/pglog*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/usr/bin/env*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/dev/null*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/tmp/kubectl*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/etc/timezone*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/usr/bin/python3*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*/var/lib/svc_internal/runner.py*" OR Filesystem.file_name IN ("kube.py","pgmon.py","proxy.sh","pcpcat.py"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — TeamPCP deploys CanisterWorm on NPM following Trivy compromise
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT

The Next Era of AppSec: Why AI-Generated Code Needs Offensive Dynamic Testing

Snyk
2026-03-20 0 use cases 1 technique 2 kill-chain phases detected

Static analysis tells you what might be vulnerable, but dynamic testing tells you what is actually exploitable. Learn why the next era of AppSec requires combining code-level context with live environment testing to secure AI-generated code.

ATT&CKT1190
Click any ATT&CK pill below to open it on the Matrix
Phase 1
Reconnaissance
Detected

[LLM] BYOVD: Genshin Impact mhyprot.sys driver dropped/loaded outside legitimate game install (Embargo evil-mhyprot-cli) Installation High
Hunts the BYOVD pattern called out in the ESET article where the Embargo gang shifted to the public evil-mhyprot-cli PoC, which abuses the Genshin Impact anti-cheat driver mhyprot.sys / mhyprot2.sys to terminate protected processes from kernel context. Signal is the driver file landing on disk or being registered as a service anywhere other than a legitimate miHoYo / Genshin Impact install path. Rationale: Article names evil-mhyprot-cli explicitly as Embargo's current EDR killer. The driver filenames mhyprot.sys / mhyprot2.sys and the SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\mhyprot key are well-documented BYOVD artefacts; flagging them outside legitimate Genshin Impact paths is high-fidelity because no business workflow drops this driver elsewhere. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/kkent030315/evil-mhyprot-cli • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/edr-killers-explained-beyond-the-drivers/ • https://www.loldrivers.io/drivers/
ATT&CKT1068 T1562.001 T1014
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry
let _legit_paths = dynamic([@"\Genshin Impact\", @"\miHoYo\", @"\HoYoPlay\"]);
union
(
  DeviceFileEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ActionType == "FileCreated"
  | where FileName in~ ("mhyprot.sys","mhyprot2.sys")
  | where not(FolderPath has_any (_legit_paths))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
            InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
            InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="FileWrite"
),
(
  DeviceRegistryEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ActionType in ("RegistryValueSet","RegistryKeyCreated")
  | where RegistryKey has @"\CurrentControlSet\Services\mhyprot"
      or (RegistryValueName =~ "ImagePath" and RegistryValueData has_any ("mhyprot.sys","mhyprot2.sys"))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RegistryKey, RegistryValueName, RegistryValueData,
            InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
            InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="ServiceReg"
)
| order by Timestamp desc
let _legit_paths = dynamic([@"\Genshin Impact\", @"\miHoYo\", @"\HoYoPlay\"]);
union isfuzzy=true
(
  ImFileEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventType == "FileCreated"
  | where TargetFileName in~ ("mhyprot.sys","mhyprot2.sys")
  | where not(TargetFilePath has_any (_legit_paths))
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
            ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername, Source="FileWrite"
),
(
  SecurityEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventID == 7045   // service installed
  | where ServiceFileName has_any ("mhyprot.sys","mhyprot2.sys")
     or ServiceName has "mhyprot"
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname=Computer, ServiceName, ServiceFileName, ServiceType, ServiceStartType, AccountName=SubjectUserName, Source="ServiceInstall"
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Genshin Impact mhyprot Vulnerable Driver Dropped Outside Legitimate Install Path
id: 8c1e1f5d-7a32-4c7e-9c1c-2bb0c6f7d8e4
status: experimental
description: Detects the Genshin Impact anti-cheat driver (mhyprot.sys / mhyprot2.sys) being written to disk outside the legitimate miHoYo / Genshin Impact / HoYoPlay install paths. This driver is abused by the evil-mhyprot-cli PoC and by the Embargo ransomware gang as a BYOVD EDR killer.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/edr-killers-explained-beyond-the-drivers/
  - https://github.com/kkent030315/evil-mhyprot-cli
  - https://www.loldrivers.io/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/03
tags:
  - attack.defense_evasion
  - attack.t1068
  - attack.t1562.001
  - attack.t1014
logsource:
  category: file_event
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    TargetFilename|endswith:
      - '\mhyprot.sys'
      - '\mhyprot2.sys'
  filter_legit:
    TargetFilename|contains:
      - '\Genshin Impact\'
      - '\miHoYo\'
      - '\HoYoPlay\'
  condition: selection and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
  - Legitimate Genshin Impact installation in a non-default directory
level: high

[LLM] EDRSilencer-style WFP filter blocking outbound traffic from named EDR binaries Installation Medium
Hunts the driverless EDR-disruption technique highlighted by ESET as gaining quick ransomware adoption: EDRSilencer (and clones) install persistent Windows Filtering Platform filters that drop outbound traffic for EDR sensor binaries (MsMpEng.exe, MsSense.exe, SentinelAgent.exe, CSFalconService.exe, ekrn.exe, etc.). Detection ties WFP block events (EID 5157) to known EDR binary paths and cross-references unusual processes loading FWPUCLNT.dll. Rationale: Article specifically calls out EDRSilencer (and EDR-Freeze) as the rapidly-adopted driverless class. Detection pivots on the technique invariant: WFP-layer drops of outbound traffic from named EDR sensor binaries — Trend Micro and Mahmoud Elfawair's hunting writeup both confirm EID 5157 + EDR application-name as the high-fidelity signal. Sigma is omitted because the detection is a count/window correlation rather than single-event-shape. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/netero1010/EDRSilencer • https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/research/24/j/edrsilencer-disrupting-endpoint-security-solutions.html • https://github.com/amjcyber/EDRNoiseMaker • https://www.csoonline.com/article/3567074/attackers-repurpose-edrsilencer-to-evade-detection.html
ATT&CKT1562.004 T1562.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let _edr_binaries = dynamic(["MsMpEng.exe","MsSense.exe","MsSenseS.exe","SenseIR.exe","SentinelAgent.exe","SentinelServiceHost.exe","CSFalconService.exe","CSFalconContainer.exe","ekrn.exe","egui.exe","elastic-agent.exe","xagt.exe","cb.exe","RepMgr.exe","bdservicehost.exe","cyserver.exe","cytray.exe","qualysagent.exe","TmListen.exe","PccNTMon.exe"]);
let _edr_binaries_lower = _edr_binaries | mv-apply b=_edr_binaries to typeof(string) on (project tolower(b));
union
(
  // Direct execution of the EDRSilencer tool / clones
  DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
  | where FileName =~ "EDRSilencer.exe"
     or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("FwpmFilterAdd0","FwpmEngineOpen0")
     or (FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe") and ProcessCommandLine has_all ("New-NetFirewallRule","Block","Outbound") and ProcessCommandLine has_any (_edr_binaries))
     or (FileName =~ "netsh.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "wfp" and ProcessCommandLine has "add")
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
            ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName,
            InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source="ToolExec"
),
(
  // Non-EDR processes loading the WFP user-mode client (FWPUCLNT.DLL) - rare outside Defender/firewall control panel
  DeviceImageLoadEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
  | where FileName =~ "FWPUCLNT.DLL"
  | where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\"
      and InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files\"
  | where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("netsh.exe","MsMpEng.exe","svchost.exe","explorer.exe","mmc.exe","WerFault.exe")
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName,
            InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
            InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="WfpDllLoad"
)
| order by Timestamp desc
let _edr_binaries = dynamic([@"\MsMpEng.exe",@"\MsSense.exe",@"\MsSenseS.exe",@"\SenseIR.exe",@"\SentinelAgent.exe",@"\SentinelServiceHost.exe",@"\CSFalconService.exe",@"\CSFalconContainer.exe",@"\ekrn.exe",@"\egui.exe",@"\elastic-agent.exe",@"\xagt.exe",@"\cb.exe",@"\RepMgr.exe",@"\bdservicehost.exe",@"\cyserver.exe",@"\TmListen.exe",@"\PccNTMon.exe"]);
// EID 5157 = WFP blocked an outbound connection. Burst of blocks for EDR binaries == EDRSilencer pattern.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where EventID in (5157, 5152)
| extend Application = tostring(extract(@"Application Name:\s*([^\r\n]+)", 1, EventData))
| where isnotempty(Application)
| where Application has_any (_edr_binaries)
| summarize BlockCount = count(),
            DistinctEdrBinaries = dcount(Application),
            BlockedApps = make_set(Application, 10),
            FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated),
            LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated)
            by Computer, bin(TimeGenerated, 10m)
| where BlockCount >= 20 or DistinctEdrBinaries >= 2
| order by LastSeen desc
ENDPOINT

[LLM] EDR-Freeze: WerFaultSecure.exe abused to suspend AV/EDR processes via MiniDumpWriteDump race Installation High
Hunts the EDR-Freeze technique that ESET highlights as a fast-adopted driverless EDR killer. The attack spawns the PPL-protected WerFaultSecure.exe to invoke MiniDumpWriteDump against an EDR/AV process (which suspends the target's threads), then suspends WerFaultSecure itself so the threads are never resumed — leaving the security agent in a coma. Signal: WerFaultSecure spawned by a non-WER parent with a PID/handle argument that resolves to a known EDR/AV binary, or NtSuspendProcess against WerFaultSecure from a non-system caller. Rationale: Article calls out EDR-Freeze by name as a rapidly-adopted driverless EDR killer. Microsoft's MAPP signature Behavior:Win32/EDRFreeze.A, Picus Security, BinaryDefense ARC Labs, and BleepingComputer all confirm the same invariant: WerFaultSecure.exe spawned by a non-WER parent with dump-style arguments, plus an NtSuspendProcess against WerFaultSecure itself. That parent-anomaly is the highest-fidelity, lowest-FP shape available for this technique today. Cross-checked against: • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/new-edr-freeze-tool-uses-windows-wer-to-suspend-security-software/ • https://www.picussecurity.com/resource/blog/edr-freeze-the-user-mode-attack-that-puts-security-into-a-coma • https://binarydefense.com/resources/blog/dont-freeze-me-out-bro-arc-labs-technical-analysis-of-edr-freeze • https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/wdsi/threats/malware-encyclopedia-description?Name=Behavior:Win32/EDRFreeze.A • https://detection.fyi/sigmahq/sigma/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_hktl_edr_freeze/
ATT&CKT1562.001 T1055
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
let _trusted_wer_parents = dynamic(["svchost.exe","wermgr.exe","werfault.exe","services.exe","smss.exe","csrss.exe","taskhostw.exe","sihost.exe"]);
let WerFaultSpawn =
    DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where FileName =~ "WerFaultSecure.exe"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ (_trusted_wer_parents)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
              ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFileName,
              ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
              ParentFolder = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
              ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
              ChildPid = ProcessId, Signal="WerFaultSecure_unusual_parent";
let SuspendOnWer =
    DeviceEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where ActionType in ("OpenProcessApiCall","SuspendThread","ProcessPrimaryTokenModified")
    | where FileName =~ "WerFaultSecure.exe"      // target was WerFaultSecure
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ (_trusted_wer_parents)
    | where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\System32"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
              InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName,
              Signal="Suspend_against_WerFaultSecure";
union WerFaultSpawn, SuspendOnWer
| order by Timestamp desc
let _trusted_wer_parents = dynamic(["svchost.exe","wermgr.exe","werfault.exe","services.exe","smss.exe","csrss.exe","taskhostw.exe","sihost.exe"]);
union isfuzzy=true
(
  SecurityEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where EventID == 4688
  | where NewProcessName endswith @"\WerFaultSecure.exe"
  | extend ParentLeaf = tostring(split(ParentProcessName, @"\")[-1])
  | where tolower(ParentLeaf) !in (_trusted_wer_parents)
  | where Account !endswith "$"
  | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account,
            ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, NewProcessId,
            Signal="WerFaultSecure_unusual_parent"
),
(
  ImProcessCreate
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
  | where TargetProcessName =~ "WerFaultSecure.exe"
  | extend ParentLeaf = tostring(split(ParentProcessName, @"\")[-1])
  | where tolower(ParentLeaf) !in (_trusted_wer_parents)
  | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
            ParentProcessName, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine,
            Signal="WerFaultSecure_unusual_parent_ASIM"
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: WerFaultSecure.exe Spawned By Unusual Parent (EDR-Freeze Technique)
id: 4f6c2a1b-3d5e-4c8a-9f2b-7e1c4d6f8a90
status: experimental
description: Detects WerFaultSecure.exe being spawned by a parent process that is not part of the normal Windows Error Reporting chain. EDR-Freeze abuses the PPL-protected WerFaultSecure.exe to call MiniDumpWriteDump against an EDR/AV process, suspending its threads, then suspends WerFaultSecure itself so the threads are never resumed. Highlighted by ESET as a rapidly-adopted driverless EDR killer.

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url

| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url

    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

AI Is Building Your Attack Surface. Are You Testing It?

Snyk
2026-03-19 1 use case 0 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

Speed has outpaced validation. With 62% of LLM-generated code testing as insecure and AI agents using undocumented APIs, legacy tools fall short. Learn how Snyk’s AI-powered dynamic testing secures your expanding attack surface.

Actions on Objectives
Likely
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-12420")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-12420")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

CISA KEV: CVE-2026-20131 — Cisco Secure Firewall Management Center (FMC) Software and Cisco Security Cloud Control (SCC) Firewall Management Deserialization of Untrusted Data Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-03-19 4 use cases 1 technique 3 kill-chain phases detected

Cisco Secure Firewall Management Center (FMC) Software and Cisco Security Cloud Control (SCC) Firewall Management contain a deserialization of untrusted data vulnerability in the web-based management interface that could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to execute arbitrary Java code as root on an affected device. Vendor: Cisco, Product: Secure Firewall Management Center (FMC). Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2026-03-22.

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-20131")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-20131")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |

    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.

    category: process_access
    product: windows

    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'

            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'

falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high

Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

            - '\csexec.exe'

[LLM] GlassWorm hardcoded C2 IP egress (45.32.150.251 / 217.69.3.152) for Stage-2 fetch and exfil Command & Control High
Detects any host reaching the GlassWorm Stage-2 / Stage-3 delivery and exfil IPs documented in the Aikido write-up. Stage-2 payload is POSTed to http://217.69.3.152/wall and Stage-3 binaries are fetched from 45.32.150.251 with an os: header. Rationale: Anchored on the four hardcoded IPs explicitly named by Aikido (45.32.150.251 serves Stage-2/3 payloads, 217.69.3.152/wall is the Stage-2 exfil endpoint, 217.69.0.159 + 45.150.34.158 listed in the pre-extracted IOC bundle). IP-level egress is a smoking gun with effectively zero legitimate use. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/glassworm-chrome-extension-rat

[LLM] GlassWorm Stage-3 RAT installation under %APPDATA%\QtCvyfVWKH\index.js Installation High

[LLM] GlassWorm Solana blockchain dead-drop C2 lookup via public RPC endpoints from Node Command & Control Medium
Detects DNS / connection activity from node.exe (or other non-browser process) to the nine public Solana RPC endpoints the GlassWorm loader cycles through to retrieve its Stage-2 URL from a transaction memo field. This is the loader's only network step before fetching Stage-2. Rationale: Aikido lists the nine exact RPC hostnames the loader cycles through; correlating them with a Node/npm/Python/VS Code parent process distinguishes GlassWorm activity from legitimate Solana developers (who would not generally hit all nine within a short polling loop and would not initiate from a preinstall hook). Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/glassworm-chrome-extension-rat

| where RemoteUrl has_any (SolanaRpc)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.exe","npm-cli.js","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","python.exe","py.exe","code.exe","electron.exe")
   or InitiatingProcessParentFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","code.exe")

| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] GlassWorm Stage-3a Ledger impersonator binary execution (SHA256 06fab21d / SKuyzYcDD.exe) Actions on Objectives High
Detects execution of the .NET WPF Ledger Live / Trezor impersonator dropped by GlassWorm's Stage-3a, either by SHA256 06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d or by the drop name SKuyzYcDD.exe in %TEMP%, or by the signed-binary metadata strings Assaac.exe / LLC LogicSub. Rationale: SHA256 06fab21d... and the drop name SKuyzYcDD.exe are explicit IOCs from the Aikido article; the LLC LogicSub / Assaac.exe publisher and original-filename strings are unique and survive renames. Combining four orthogonal anchors (hash, drop name, %TEMP% path, signed metadata) keeps the detection resilient to single-IOC rotation. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/glassworm-chrome-extension-rat
ATT&CKT1036.005 T1657 T1555
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 == "06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d"
   or FileName =~ "SKuyzYcDD.exe"
   or (FolderPath has @"\Temp\" and FileName endswith ".exe" and ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName =~ "Assaac.exe")
   or ProcessVersionInfoCompanyName =~ "LLC LogicSub"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine, ProcessVersionInfoCompanyName, ProcessVersionInfoOriginalFileName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 == "06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d"
   or TargetProcessName endswith @"\SKuyzYcDD.exe"
   or ActingProcessName endswith @"\SKuyzYcDD.exe"
   or (TargetProcessCommandLine has @"\Temp\" and TargetProcessCommandLine has_cs "SKuyzYcDD")
   or ActingProcessFileCompany =~ "LLC LogicSub"
   or TargetProcessFileCompany =~ "LLC LogicSub"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, SHA256, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessFileCompany
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/

author: Clankerusecase

    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003

    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'

        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'

    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"

              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;

| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)

// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;

| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase

          CommandLine, ParentProcessName

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d") or SHA1 in~ ("06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d") or MD5 in~ ("06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d") or SHA1 in~ ("06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d") or MD5 in~ ("06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["06fab21dc276e3ab9b5d0a1532398979fd377b080c86d74f2c53a04603a43b1d"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

fast-draft Open VSX Extension Compromised by BlokTrooper

Aikido
2026-03-18 12 use cases 4 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

The fast-draft Open VSX extension was compromised to deploy a BlokTrooper RAT and infostealer via GitHub-hosted payloads. Multiple malicious versions identified. Category: Vulnerabilities & Threats

ATT&CKT1027 T1059.001 T1190 T1555.003
Domainsraw.githubusercontent.com/BlokTrooper/extension
IPs195.201.104.53

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] VSCode/VSCodium spawning shell or curl to raw.githubusercontent.com/BlokTrooper · [LLM] VSCode-family host fetching from raw.githubusercontent.com/BlokTrooper/extension path
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Four-way node.exe -e fanout spawned from VSCode shell descendants (BlokTrooper stage-2) · Article-specific behavioural hunt — fast-draft Open VSX Extension Compromised by BlokTrooper
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Outbound TCP beacon to BlokTrooper Socket.IO C2 195.201.104.53:6931/6936/6939 · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Non-browser process copying Chrome/Edge/Brave Login Data, Web Data, or wallet extension LevelDB state · Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
[LLM] VSCode/VSCodium spawning shell or curl to raw.githubusercontent.com/BlokTrooper Delivery High
Detects the fast-draft Open VSX delivery primitive: a VSCode-family editor (Code.exe, VSCodium.exe, Cursor.exe) or its bundled node.exe spawning curl/cmd/sh/powershell whose command line references the BlokTrooper staging repo on raw.githubusercontent.com. Hits 0.10.89, 0.10.105, 0.10.106 and 0.10.112 alike. Rationale: Anchors on the exact staging URL fragment 'raw.githubusercontent.com/BlokTrooper' named in the article and ties it to a VSCode-family parent — extension hosts legitimately shell out for many reasons, but never to this attacker-controlled repo. Cross-checked against Aikido write-up plus general supply-chain TTPs. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/fast-draft-open-vsx-bloktrooper • https://open-vsx.org/extension/KhangNghiem/fast-draft • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/002/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.003 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Code.exe","Code - Insiders.exe","VSCodium.exe","Cursor.exe","node.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("curl.exe","cmd.exe","sh.exe","bash.exe","wsl.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "raw.githubusercontent.com" and ProcessCommandLine has "BlokTrooper"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("Code.exe","Code - Insiders.exe","VSCodium.exe","Cursor.exe","node.exe")
